## Session 27 · builder · F012 Grab the bottle and push it upward with the mouse to throw
- Did:
  - Core, no Phaser and no clock. `src/core/grab.ts`: `isOnBottle(x, y, bottle, padding)` (a press is "on the bottle" inside the box around the whole bottle plus `grabPadding`, and follows the bottle's angle) and `Grab` (the samples of one drag, `power()` for the live push, `finish()` for the release, `cancel()`; it keeps only the samples the push can still see). `src/core/mouseControl.ts`: `MouseControl`, exposed as `sim.mouse` (`canGrab`, `grab`, `drag`, `release`, `cancel`, `bottleLeft`, `step`, `dragging`), reaching the Simulation only through a small `MouseHost`. `Simulation` owns it: `step()` calls `mouse.step()`, `startFlight()` calls `mouse.bottleLeft()` (a keyboard throw or `placeBottle` ends a grab), and the snapshot has `dragging`. I first wrote these as methods on `Simulation` (334 lines), then extracted them to keep it under the 300-line guideline (now 280).
  - New tuning (the panel lists them by itself): `pushMinPower` 1 (a release under it throws nothing) and `grabPadding` 12 px.
  - Behavior I chose where the feature left it open (all in D25 in `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`): the press sets the bar to the live push, which is 0; the bar then shows `pushPower` of the drag at every move; `Simulation.step()` counts steps with no pointer event, so a pointer that stops makes the bar fade to 0 over 150 ms (a push that is not still moving would otherwise show a power the release won't throw). A release throws through `throwBottle(power, 'mouse')` and the bar keeps that power. A release below `pushMinPower` drops the grab and puts the bar back to what it showed before the grab. A release outside the canvas (Phaser's `pointerupoutside`) has no position the game can trust (Phaser sees no moves there), so the pointer counts as where it was last seen. Space during a drag throws with the live power as a keyboard throw and ends the grab. A window blur drops the grab.
  - Scene: `src/game/gameInput.ts` forwards the pointer (left press = grab, otherwise `skip()` as before; moves; `pointerup`; `pointerupoutside`; blur) with the DOM event's own `timeStamp` as the time. New `src/game/view/cursorView.ts` (`cursorFor(dragging, overGrabbable)`, pure, plus a view that sets `canvas.style.cursor`) called from `GameScene.draw()`.
  - `npm run snap`: new actions `mousedown:x;y`, `mousemove:x;y;ms` and `mouseup`; the mouse position carries over between actions so a drag can be photographed halfway. The drag action now shares the same `glide` helper. `--help` has an example and the gotcha that a release throws only if the mouse was still moving in the last 150 ms (do a short `mousemove` after a shot, before `mouseup`).
  - Docs: `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` (directory rows for `grab.ts`, `mouseControl.ts`, `cursorView.ts`, the snap actions and the new tests, the snapshot sketch, D18's note, new D25).
  - Tests (none changed or removed): unit `grab.test.ts` (19: hit box on every side with and without padding, a turned bottle, the `Grab` samples, fade, clock running backward, long drags), `mouseThrow.test.ts` (grab only while aiming, the live bar, every way a release throws or doesn't, Space and `placeBottle` during a drag, cancel, and the keyboard-vs-mouse equivalence: same power, water and seed give the same snapshot step by step for 3 pushes x 3 water levels, plus a hold in the hand first), `cursorView.test.ts`, and 2 more in `tests/harness/snapActions.test.ts`. E2E `mouse.spec.ts` (6: the 200 px / 100 ms push throws; the same with Playwright's real mouse; the live bar equals `pushPower` of the logged events at every move; a push on a virtual clock; fast and long vs slow and short; a stopped pointer fades the bar and throws nothing) and `mouseNothing.spec.ts` (9: press away, click without moving, push down or a jitter, a drag while flying, a click during the result, release outside the canvas, a flick released outside, window blur; and the cursor).
- Verified:
  - `npm run verify` green: format, typecheck, 445 unit tests in 34 files, build, 92 e2e tests (77 before). The two mouse specs also passed 3 times in a row (45/45) after the last change.
  - Acceptance 1: `mouse.spec.ts` "pressing on the bottle, pushing 200 px up in about 100 ms and releasing throws it" (phase flying, `lastThrow.source` 'mouse', power about 83, equal to the bar and to `pushPower` of the page's own event log) and the same with Playwright's real mouse. 2: "a fast, long push gives more power than a slow, short one" (about 100 vs 7.5) and "while the button is held, state().power follows the live push" (equal to the expected power after every move, and the bar's label shows it). 3: `mouseNothing.spec.ts`, one test per case listed in the criterion. 4: `mouseThrow.test.ts` "a mouse throw and a keyboard throw are the same throw". 5: "the cursor is a grab hand over the held bottle and a grabbing hand while dragging" (computed style `grab`, `grabbing`, `auto`), and a snap: `npm run snap -- --url "/?seed=7" --do "pause,mousedown:280;410,mousemove:280;250;100,shot:mid-drag,..."` showed the bottle still in the hand and the bar at 27% while `state().power` was 26.8; a following short move and `mouseup` threw it (bar kept at 14%, bottle in the air, spinning).
  - I checked that the outside-release tests can fail: with the `pointerupoutside` handler removed, both failed (dragging stayed true / no throw); restored.
- Status: F012 → needs_review
- Issues / follow-ups:
  - Timing in e2e: headless Chromium draws in software, so Playwright's mouse takes tens of ms per call (a "100 ms" glide measured 500 to 700 ms, with 35 to 100 ms between events) and in-page timers stall behind frames. A first version that asserted push strength from real-time moves failed 1 time in 3. The tests that need a push of a given speed now use `sendMouse` / `dragInPage` in `tests/e2e/mouseHelpers.ts`, which dispatch mouse events at once with a `timeStamp` of their own (a virtual clock). Phaser and the game never check that events are trusted; the real mouse is still used for what is grabbed, the cursor and the release outside. Use the same helpers for any later test about push speed.
  - A real mouse's events arrive about once per frame, and on a slow machine more than 150 ms apart: each move is then a push of its own (the game measures the last 150 ms only), by design (D21).
  - Press position: a press outside the box around the bottle plus 12 px does nothing in aiming (and `skip()` does nothing there either). The box includes the empty corners beside the neck and cap. A tighter silhouette hit test is possible if the human wants it.
  - Not done, out of scope: touch input; right and middle buttons never grab (they still end a result wait on press, as before).
  - Process: compound shell commands writing to `/tmp` and a background `npm run verify` were denied automatically; I ran the verify in the foreground and split files with `sed` inside the repo. A `node -e` script full of backticks broke on shell quoting, so I used the Edit tool.
- Next: F013 (rounds) can rely on `sim.mouse.dragging` / `snapshot().dragging` for "the player is mid-throw" and on `throws` being counted at release. If F014's screens or a pause overlay cover the canvas, call `sim.mouse.cancel()` when they open.

- Did:
  - Core: new `src/core/round.ts` (`Round`: the round's throws, score, landings and streak; `released()`, `judged(outcome)`, `reset()`, `complete`, `throwNumber(holding)`; `roundLength(tuning)` reads `throwsPerRound` as a whole number of at least 1). New tuning `throwsPerRound: 10`. `Simulation` owns a `Round`; a result wait that ends (by time or `skip()`) goes to the next bottle, or to the new phase `roundOver` after the last throw (no bottle is brought, the last one stays on the table, `outcome` is null). `Simulation.restartRound()` works from every phase: drops a mouse grab, resets the round, destroys the current bottle and puts a new one in the hand. Snapshot gains `throwNumber`, `throwsPerRound`, `landings`, `streak`; `Phase` gains `'roundOver'`. I moved `landingSample()` from `Simulation` into `Bottle` to keep `simulation.ts` under 300 lines (297).
  - Scene: R now calls `sim.restartRound()` (in `gameInput.ts`, ignoring auto-repeat) on the same Simulation. It used to start a new scene with `seed + 1`; the seed now stays and the water sequence carries on, as the feature's note assumed. `scoreView.ts` became `hudView.ts` (three texts in a top-left stack: "Throw 3 / 10" 28 px, "Score: 2" 40 px, "Streak: 1" 28 px) with the words in the pure `hudText.ts`. Hook `window.__FLIPPER__.restartRound()`.
  - Decisions (D26 in `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`): `score`, `landings` and `streak` are the round's and reset on restart; `throws` stays the count of every release in the simulation's life (the tuning panel's `LastThrowTracker` uses it to tell decided throws apart, so a reset would hide the first throw after a restart), and `throwNumber` is the round's own counter. `restartRound` keeps the power bar, `lastThrow`, the water lock and the clock.
  - Tests (none removed): unit `round.test.ts` (Round and roundLength), `roundPlay.test.ts` (scripted 10-throw round: score = sum of turns with a double adding 2, landings, a fail resets the streak, throwNumber 1 to 10; roundOver after the 10th wait and by `skip()`; throwBottle, placeBottle, key, mouse and skip do nothing there), `restartRound.test.ts` (from aiming, mid-flight, during a result and after the end; mouse grab; seed's water sequence continues; determinism), `hudText.test.ts`; helpers `play` and `playRound` added to `tests/unit/helpers.ts`. E2E `round.spec.ts` (7). One existing test, `tuningPanel.spec.ts` "typing digits, Space, letters or arrow keys into a field...", changed: it asserted that R did not restart the game by checking the seed, which R no longer changes, so it would have passed whatever R did. It now plays one landing first and asserts `throwNumber` 2, `score` 1, `landings` 1 and `throws` unchanged afterwards (stronger, not weaker).
- Verified:
  - `npm run verify` green: format, typecheck, 476 unit tests in 38 files (445 before), build, 99 e2e (92 before). The unit tests had passed unchanged right after the core change, before I added any test.
  - Acceptance 1: `roundPlay.test.ts` "scores the turns of each success, counts landings and resets the streak on a fail" (the sums are written out by hand: 1, 1, 3, 4, 4, 7, 7, 7, 8, 10) and "counts the throw number from 1 to 10"; "is kept by real throws too" checks every number against the outcomes the judge gave. 2: "is reached after the 10th result's wait" (phase `result` for 89 steps after the outcome, `roundOver` on the 90th, bottle not in the hand), "is reached at once by skip()", "refuses every throw". 3: `restartRound.test.ts`, one test per phase. 4: `round.spec.ts` "the HUD shows Throw 1 / 10, Score: 0 and Streak: 0 at the start", "updates after each throw" and "pressing R mid-round puts the HUD back to the start" (plus R mid-flight, during a result, after the round ended). 5: snaps below.
  - Mutation checks: with the R handler turned into a no-op, the two R e2e tests failed; with the panel's `stopPropagation` guard removed, the strengthened tuning panel test failed (on the power assertion, which comes first; the R assertion comes after it). Both restored. My first version of that panel test had a typo (`FLIPPED` used inside `page.evaluate`), which made it fail for the wrong reason until I passed it as an argument, so I re-did the mutation check after the fix.
  - Snaps at 1280x720, `/?seed=7`, paused (`call:setWater;0.5`, `call:throwBottle;44`, `key:KeyR` and so on, looked at with Read): the HUD is a stack at the top left, x 32 to about 215 and y 22 to about 140, readable on the light background, and clear of the power bar (its caption starts at y 222) and of the held bottle (x 230 to 330, y 347 to 460). After the 10th result and its wait: "Throw 10 / 10", Score 7, Streak 2, the last bottle still on the table and nothing in the hand; a throw by hook, a click on where the bottle would be and Space changed nothing (`phase` stayed `roundOver`). R gave Throw 1 / 10, Score 0, Streak 0 and a new bottle with different water. No console errors.

- Issues / follow-ups:

## Session 31 · builder · F014 Round summary screen
- Did:
  - Scene: new `SummaryScene` (`src/game/scenes/SummaryScene.ts`, registered in `config.ts`). `GameScene.update` opens it when `sim.phase === 'roundOver'`: it lets go of held keys and any mouse grab, launches `Summary` with `{ sim }` and pauses itself. A paused Phaser scene gets no input (`canInput()` is false after RUNNING), which is what silences the Game scene's keys and mouse while the summary is up, and it freezes the last bottle (so the "falls forever" note from F013 is moot). Play again (click, Enter, Space or R) calls `sim.restartRound()` on the same simulation, resumes `Game` and stops `Summary`; the same simulation means the seed's water sequence carries on (D26). The summary also closes by itself if the phase is no longer `roundOver` (a hook called `restartRound()`). Key choices ignore `event.repeat`.
  - Views: `summaryView.ts` (a white card with a soft shadow, centered on the canvas, on an opaque light camera background: "Round over", "Score: N" at 96 px, "Landed N / 10", the button, and "or press Enter or Space"), `summaryText.ts` (the words, pure), `summaryLayout.ts` (constants only, so e2e tests click the button from them) and a reusable `buttonView.ts` (`createButton`: blue face on a darker lip, lighter on hover, darker and sunk while pressed, hand cursor; a click needs the press AND the release on the button, so the click that skipped the last result never presses it; `buttonLook` is pure). Theme gains card and button colors (white on the face is 3.7:1 or better in every state).
  - Hooks: `startScene` now stops paused scenes too (the Game scene is paused while the summary is open); `activeScenes()` lists running scenes only, so with the summary open it is `["Summary"]` and `texts()` shows only the summary's texts (that keeps "Score: N" in `texts()` unambiguous: the HUD's own "Score: N" is not in the list).
  - Tests (none removed): unit `summaryText.test.ts`, `buttonLook.test.ts` (look per state, layout inside the card); e2e `summary.spec.ts` (7) and `summaryInput.spec.ts` (7), shared pieces in `summaryHelpers.ts` and `roundHelpers.ts` (`land`, `next`, `playThrows`, `activeScenes`, moved out of `round.spec.ts`). The scripted round has singles, doubles, a triple and two kinds of fail: 11 points from 7 landings.
  - Changed existing tests, with the reason: in `round.spec.ts` "after the 10th throw the round is over..." no longer presses Space at `roundOver` (Space now chooses Play again, by this feature's design) and, with the "10th result can be skipped" test, checks the Summary's "Score: N" / "Landed N / 10" instead of the HUD lines, which are in the paused Game scene and no longer visible. ## Session 33 · builder · F015 Save the best round score in the browser
- Did:
  - Core: `updateBest(previousBest, score)` in `src/core/round.ts` returns `{ best, isNewBest }`. Strictly higher is a new best, a tie or lower is not, and with no saved best (0) any score above 0 is one (a score of 0 never is). A best or score that is not finite or is below 0 counts as 0.
  - Storage: new `src/game/storage.ts`. One entry, `SAVE_KEY = 'flipper.save.v1'`, holding `{ version: 1, best }`. `parseSave(text)` gives the defaults (`{ best: 0 }`) for a missing entry, invalid JSON, a non-object, another version, or a `best` that is not a safe integer of at least 0 (each field falls back on its own, ready for F017's mute). `SaveStore(storage)` has `load()` (reads afresh every time), `update(changes)` (merges, keeps other fields) and `recordRound(score)` (calls `updateBest`, writes only for a new best). It never throws; with no usable storage (null or one that throws) it remembers what it last wrote, so the best still holds for the rest of the visit instead of every round being a "New best!". `browserStorage()` returns null where even touching `window.localStorage` throws; `saveStore()` is the shared instance.
  - Summary: `SummaryScene.create` calls `saveStore().recordRound(score)` once, when the summary opens (not on Play again), and passes `best` and `isNewBest` to the view. `summaryText.ts` gains `bestLine` ("Best: N") and `newBestLine` ("New best!" or null); `summaryView.ts` draws "Best: N" (36 px) under "Landed" and, only for a new best, a green "New best!" (32 px, `successText`) under it. `summaryLayout.ts`: card 460 → 560 px tall (still centered, 80 to 640), new `bestY` 358 and `newBestY` 398, everything below moved down (Play again at y 488, key hint at 586). Tests click the button from the layout, so they followed by themselves.
  - Tests (none removed or weakened): unit `best.test.ts` (7), `saveStore.test.ts` (24: parseSave cases, the store with a Map-backed Storage, a throwing Storage and no storage, recordRound across "visits", `browserStorage` with a stubbed window); `summaryText.test.ts` now builds its state with a helper (the new fields are required) and adds the best-line cases; `buttonLook.test.ts` layout test now also orders `bestY` and `newBestY` and checks their spacing. E2E `bestScore.spec.ts` (9): 3 points shows Best: 3 and New best!; the entry is `{ version: 1, best: 3 }` under `flipper.save.v1` (and is the only `flipper.save*` key); the story 3, reload, 2 (Best: 3, no note), play again, 12 from six doubles (Best: 12, New best!), reload, 1 (Best: 12, no note); a tie is not new; a 0-point round shows Best: 0 and no note; a full 10-throw round (11 points); a damaged entry and a version-2 entry are ignored and replaced; with `Storage.prototype` blocked the game plays without errors and keeps the best for the visit. Rounds are shortened with `setTuningValue('throwsPerRound', n)`. Docs: `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` (round.ts row, view row, new storage row, D28).
- Verified:
  - `npm run verify` exit 0: format check, typecheck, 520 unit tests in 42 files (487 in 40 before), build, 122 e2e (113 before) in 6.5 min.
  - Acceptance 1: `best.test.ts` ("treats a higher score as a new best", "does not treat an equal score...", "does not treat a lower score...", "counts any score above 0 as a new best when there is no saved best"). 2: `saveStore.test.ts` ("gives the defaults for a missing entry", "...for text that is not valid JSON", "loads the defaults, and never throws, from a Storage that throws"). 3: the story test in `bestScore.spec.ts` ("the best score carries over a reload: 3, then 2 (no new best), then 12 (new best)"). 4: "the best score is stored under the versioned localStorage key".
  - Mutation check: changing `>` to `>=` in `updateBest` failed five unit tests (tie, zero, damaged input, and the store's "equal or lower" and "does not write" tests); restored.
  - Snaps (a throwaway Playwright spec that played rounds and called `page.screenshot`, deleted afterwards) at 1280x720 and 1024x576: new best (Score 3, Best: 3, green "New best!" under it), kept (Score 2, Best: 3, empty row where the note would be, the gap above Play again is a little larger) and 90 points (Score: 90, Best: 90, New best!). Nothing overlaps or is cut off, the card sits centered with its shadow on the screen, the button and key hint are unchanged apart from moving down.
- Status: F015 → needs_review
- Issues / follow-ups:
  - When a round is not a new best, the "New best!" row stays empty, so the card has a gap between "Best: N" and the button. It reads fine, but F018 (Title button) may want to rebalance the card.
  - `SaveStore.update` merges into what `load()` returns, so two tabs writing at once can overwrite each other's best by a race; the best only ever goes up through `recordRound` (it re-reads first), so the window is tiny. Not worth more.
  - If a storage reads fine but refuses writes (quota), nothing is remembered at all (the in-memory fallback is only used when reading fails). That is rare and harmless: the game still plays.
  - The title screen (F018) should show "Best: N" from `saveStore().load().best`; the design wants it there too.
  - A full 10-throw e2e round varies between 8 and 18 s on this machine under load (the default timeout is 30 s); the other tests here use 1 to 6 throws.
  - Process: a compound command with a `/tmp` redirect, a heredoc and a variable-setting `Monitor` command were auto-denied; I used the Write/Edit tools and single commands, and waited for verify with a background `until grep` loop (my first pattern matched Vitest's "42 passed (42)" and ended early; it needs `passed \(` followed by a duration).
- Next: F016 and F017 can add their fields to `SaveData` and `parseSave` in `src/game/storage.ts` (mute for F017) and read or write them through `saveStore().load()` / `update({...})`; F018's Title screen can show the best with `saveStore().load().best`.

No source change was needed.
  - Core: `src/core/events.ts` has the `SimEvent` types (`throw` with power and source, `impact` with the approach speed in m/s, `outcome` with result, reason and points, each with its `tick`) and `SimEvents`, the queue the Simulation owns. Impacts come from planck's begin-contact between the table and the bottle: the speed of the contact point into the table before the solver changes it, the hardest hit of a step as one event, so a resting bottle gives none. `Simulation.drainEvents()` hands the queue over; `throwBottle` queues a throw (`placeBottle` does not), `showResult` queues the one outcome. Tuning gained `knockMinSpeed` 0.35, `knockFullSpeed` 8 and `knockGapSeconds` 0.1.
  - Audio (`src/game/audio/`): `soundPlan.ts` is pure (`SoundPlanner.plan(events, tuning)` gives whoosh, knock, chime or thud cues with a volume; knocks below the minimum speed are dropped, and so is any within 100 ms of the last knock that sounded, counted in the events' ticks); `synth.ts` makes the four voices from oscillators and filtered noise; `sfx.ts` has `Sfx` (owns the one AudioContext, created or resumed only by `unlock()`, logs every cue, never throws) and `unlockOnFirstInput` (first keydown, pointerdown or pointerup, capture phase; `main.ts` installs it). `GameScene.update` drains the events once per frame and plays the cues. `config.ts` turns Phaser's own sound manager off so it makes no second context. `window.__FLIPPER__.sounds()` returns the log (name, volume, tick).
  - Tests (none removed): unit `simEvents.test.ts`, `soundPlan.test.ts`, `sfx.test.ts`, `synth.test.ts` with `fakeAudio.ts`; e2e `sound.spec.ts` (11) and `soundRender.spec.ts` (4), helpers in `soundHelpers.ts`. Docs: `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` (events, audio, hooks and test rows, D29).
- Verified:
  - `npm run format`, then `npm run verify` exit 0: format check, typecheck, 624 unit tests in 46 files, build, 137 e2e in 6.5 min.
  - Acceptance 1: `simEvents.test.ts` (throw with power and source, not for a refused throw or `placeBottle`; impact with its speed, one per step, not for a bottle at rest, in the hand or in the air; exactly one outcome per throw over a whole round). 2: `sound.spec.ts` "before any input there is no sound and no AudioContext...", "the first key press creates one running AudioContext..." and "the first click creates one running AudioContext, and does not throw the bottle" (a tracker on `window.AudioContext` in the page, not the game's hook). 3: "a Space throw plays a whoosh, then knocks when it lands, then a chime for an upright landing" and "a failed Space throw plays a whoosh, a knock and a thud, and no chime", plus a real-time throw. 4: `soundPlan.test.ts` (exactly 100 ms counts as too close, a dropped knock does not restart the gap, same answer however the events are split into frames) and e2e "an impact below the minimum speed makes no knock...", "the minimum impact speed is in the tuning..." and "no two knocks sound within 100 ms...". 5: every test in `sound.spec.ts` asserts that the page's console errors and a separate list of audio or sound console messages are empty, and the rest of the suite asserts no console errors.
  - Snap (`npm run snap -- --url "/?seed=7&testhooks" --do "pause,shot:start,key:Space,step:100,shot:after"`): the game looks as before, the bottle is mid-flight after the Space throw, and the snap reported no console errors. Sound can't be seen in a screenshot, so the e2e tests and the offline renders in `soundRender.spec.ts` (audible, never clipped, ends in silence, thud low and chime high, knock short and chime longest) are the evidence for how it sounds. I could not listen to it.

## Session 39 · builder · F017 Mute toggle that is remembered
- Did:
  - State and gate: `Sfx` (`src/game/audio/sfx.ts`) holds `muted`, `setMuted(muted)` and `onMutedChange(listener)`. While muted `play` returns first thing, so a muted cue is neither made nor logged (`sounds()` gets nothing), and the master gain is 0, which cuts a sound already ringing. A context created while muted starts at gain 0 and is still unlocked, so unmuting needs no second click. `src/game/audio/mute.ts` is the only code joining `Sfx` to the save: `restoreMute()` (called in `main.ts` before the first input), `setMute(muted)` and `toggleMute()`.
  - Save: `SaveData` and `parseSave` in `src/game/storage.ts` gained `muted` (false by default, any non-boolean dropped on its own, so a good `muted` beside a damaged `best` is kept). The key and version did not change: an F015 entry with no `muted` reads as not muted.
  - Control: `createMuteControl(scene)` (`src/game/view/muteButtonView.ts`) draws a 56 px speaker button in the top-right corner (`muteLayout.ts`) and binds M (auto-repeat ignored); `GameScene` calls it. The icon is drawn in code (`speakerIcon.ts`, pure shape data: waves when the sound is on, a cross when muted) through a new `icon` option on `createButton` (plus `radius`, `name`, `refresh()` and the `zone` it exposes; the label is now optional). The icon follows `Sfx.onMutedChange`, so the Game scene paused behind the summary is never stale.
  - Press-through: Phaser emits the scene-level `pointerdown` for every press, with the objects under it, so `bindGameInput` now returns when that list is not empty. Without this a click on the speaker skipped a result (the mutation check below shows it).
  - Hooks: `muted()`, `setMuted(muted)` (as the key does, saved) and `muteButton()` (the icon it last drew and its clickable area in game pixels; `src/debug/visibleMuteButton.ts` finds the zone by name).

## Session 43 · builder · F019 Pause overlay

- Did:
  - Pause overlay as a scene, the same pattern as the summary (D27): `PauseScene` (`src/game/scenes/PauseScene.ts`) is launched over the Game scene, which pauses itself, so a paused scene hears no keys, wheel or mouse and steps nothing. Esc, P (`src/game/pauseKeys.ts`, auto-repeat ignored) and a corner button open it from `GameScene.openPause()`; Esc, P, Resume and the corner button close it. Restart calls `sim.restartRound()` and resumes. Quit to title is the new shared `quitToTitle(scene)` (`src/game/scenes/toTitle.ts`: stop Game, start Title), which the Summary's Title button now uses too (the helper F018 suggested).
  - `openPause()` lets go of held power keys and a mouse grab first (their release would never be heard while paused, so a held Up would keep raising the bar after the resume), refuses when the round is over (the summary opens instead) and when a pause is already on its way (`pauseRequested`, which also stops the Game scene stepping from the key press on).
  - Views (`src/game/view/`): `pauseView.ts` (a dark veil, a white card with "Paused", Resume, Restart and Quit to title stacked, "Esc or P to resume"), `pauseButtonView.ts` (the corner button left of the speaker; two bars while playing, a play triangle on the overlay where it resumes), `pauseIcon.ts`, `pauseText.ts`, `pauseLayout.ts` (constants, imported by the e2e tests to click), and `iconDraw.ts` (the shape-drawing loop moved out of `muteButtonView.ts` so both buttons share it). The overlay also calls `createMuteControl`, so the speaker and M work while paused. The veil is a Graphics fill, not a Rectangle, because `visibleFlash` takes any canvas-sized Rectangle for the result flash.
  - `createButton` lets go of its hover and pressed state on its scene's `pause` event. Without it a button under the pointer stays lit after the overlay closes, until the mouse moves (seen in a snap: after clicking the corner button and then Resume, the corner button was lighter than the speaker while the pointer was mid-screen; fixed, checked in a second snap). The summary opening over the Game scene had the same flaw; it is fixed too.
  - Hook: `window.__FLIPPER__.gamePaused()` (the Pause scene is running), separate from `pause()` and `resume()` (the test clock) and false while the summary is open. `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`: the scenes, views, hooks and tests rows, the plan row and D32.
  - Tests (none changed or removed): `tests/unit/pauseOverlay.test.ts` (16: the words, the two icons, the corner button beside the speaker and clear of it, the card and the stacked buttons inside the canvas, in order and clear of each other), and 30 e2e: `pause.spec.ts` (15: Esc, P, mixed and the corner button; a held Esc pauses once; Esc and P in one frame; the overlay's texts and no result flash; a backdrop click and the cursor; clicking the pause or speaker button in aiming, during a result and on the last result throws, skips and ends nothing; no pause on the Title or the summary, nor when the round ends in the same frame), `pauseFreeze.spec.ts` (7: a bottle in flight frozen over 1 s of real time with the test clock running, then going on to an outcome; the same flight, step for step, as one never paused; a result wait held; Space, ArrowUp, ArrowDown, a held Up, R, the wheel and a push on the bottle change nothing, tick included; the same input works after the resume; a held Up and a grab on the bottle are dropped by the pause), `pauseActions.spec.ts` (8: Resume; Restart from throw 3 and from a bottle in the air; Quit to title with Best still 0, then Play and a new pause; the speaker and M on the overlay), helpers in `pauseHelpers.ts`.
- Verified:
  - `npm run format`, then `npm run verify` exit 0 (read from the report: format check, typecheck, 213 e2e in 12.2 min, 183 before and +30). After verify I changed only two comments in `buttonView.ts`, then ran `npm run format`, `npm run typecheck` and `npm test` (699 unit tests in 53 files; 683 in 52 before, +16), all green.
  - Mutation checks on the e2e tests (all reverted; no `MUTATION-CHECK` marker is left in `src`): without `powerMeter.release()` in `openPause`, "a held Up key does not keep raising the power" fails (power 44.6, expected 20.2); without `mouse.cancel()`, "a grab on the bottle is dropped" fails (`dragging` true); without the `roundOver` check, "a pause key as the round ends does not open the overlay over the summary" fails. Two did not fail the test I expected: removing the `pauseRequested` check from `openPause` leaves "Esc and P in the same frame" green, because Phaser copes with a second queued launch and pause (the flag still stops stepping from the key press on, which no test can see); and removing the `pause` listener from `createButton` leaves "the hand cursor does not stay on the backdrop" green, because creating the overlay's buttons resets the cursor. That cursor test still checks a real behavior; the stale lit button is only checked by the two snaps above, since no hook shows a button's hover.
  - Criterion 1: `pause.spec.ts` "Esc pauses and Esc resumes", "P pauses and P resumes", "Esc pauses and P resumes, and the other way round", "the corner pause button pauses, and the same button resumes", and the three "clicking the pause or the speaker button" tests (a whole-snapshot comparison during a result, so a skip or a throw would show). 2: `pauseFreeze.spec.ts` "freezes while paused, and the flight goes on to an outcome after the resume" (state `toEqual` after `waitForTimeout(1000)` with the test clock running, then the phase leaves `flying`) and "resumes exactly where it stopped" (interrupted and uninterrupted flights give equal snapshots at the outcome). 3: "Space, ArrowUp, the wheel, R and a push on the bottle change nothing" (the whole snapshot is equal, tick included) and "after the resume the same input works again". 4: `pauseActions.spec.ts` "starts a fresh, unpaused round: throw 1, no points" (throw 1, score 0, the tick runs again, Space throws) and "ends the round and opens the Title scene". 5: snaps (`npm run snap -- --url "/?seed=7" --do "scene:Game,pause,key:Escape,wait:300,shot:paused"`, a mid-flight one with `call:throwBottle;70,step:20,key:KeyP` at `--width 1024 --height 576`, and one over a result): the game is dimmed behind a white card, "Paused" and the three buttons are centered with clear gaps and white text on blue, the hint line is under them, the corner shows the play triangle beside the speaker, the frozen bottle is visible dimmed, and after P the bottle is in the same place with the pause bars back. No console errors in any snap.

## Human session · independent Gemini review of M4 (2026-11-18)
- What: a second-opinion review of M4 (F016 to F019, commits `7c2a133..22ecc00`) by Gemini 3.8 Flash (high) in Google Antigravity, run by hand outside the harness and read-only. It ran targeted unit tests (no regressions), checked core purity, file sizes and the test diff, took snaps, and checked every criterion. Verdict: pass with issues, all 18 criteria met. Claude then checked each finding against the code.
- Findings and decisions:
  1. Summary screen has no mute control: **fix.** `SummaryScene` never calls `createMuteControl`; with the summary open the paused Game scene's speaker is drawn but deaf and M does nothing. The F017, F018 and F019 notes had flagged it, but no feature owned it. F017 is set back to `todo` with `review_feedback` and a new acceptance criterion (M and the speaker work on the summary).
  2. A sound that is already playing finishes over the pause overlay or the title after Pause or Quit to title: **no fix.** Every sound is a one-shot under about 1 s, and mute still cuts it at once through the master gain.
  3. R doesn't restart while paused: **no fix, by design.** F019's description lists R among the inputs ignored while paused, and `pauseFreeze.spec.ts` checks it. Restart while paused is the overlay's Restart button.
  4. `unlockOnFirstInput` keeps its listeners for one more input because `AudioContext.resume()` is async: **no fix.** The extra call is a harmless no-op, and the listeners then remove themselves.
- Process notes:
  - The Gemini CLI can't be used: Google refuses sign-in with a Google account ("no longer supported for Gemini Code Assist for individuals"), and an API key bills per request. Gemini reviews stay manual, through Antigravity at milestone stops, on the Google AI Pro plan.
  - In Antigravity, `npm run verify` hung in the Playwright step (the second web server never started), so tell it not to run verify or e2e; the harness gate already does.

## Human session · outside review of gameplay, graphics and features (2026-11-18)
- What: Gemini (Antigravity, three subagents: gameplay, new features, graphics) reviewed the game read-only. Claude checked each claim against the code and, at the human's request, adopted the suggestions that are clearly better than the design. Details and reasons are in `docs/GAME_DESIGN.md` section 13, "Decided after an outside review".
- Confirmed and acted on:
  1. **One fixed power lands most bottles.** `scripts/flip-windows.ts` matches the review: power 46 lands 7 of the 9 levels. F020's planned new-player model (one fixed power at the average center, σ 8) lands about 55% against its 20-40% target, and a search of about 400 tunings found no fix inside the 1.5-2x power-ratio target. Design section 3 now asks for 2.5-4x, windows at least 10 wide, and no fixed power landing more than about 60% of levels. One tuning that meets every target (shellDensity 0.32, throwLinearImpulse 2.06, throwAngularImpulse 4.13, throwSpinGrowth 0.0153, bottleAngularDamping 2.59) was checked against `calibration.test.ts`: only the ratio and width tests failed, which F020 now updates. It is recorded in F020 as a starting point; its flight is a little lower than today's.
  2. **Dead time.** Success hold 1 s → 0.5 s (an upright, still bottle can't fall), result delay 1.5 s → 1 s. New feature F030, before F020.
  3. **Visuals.** A bottle shadow, a water surface line, a table edge, a gradient background, a gentler flash (0.4 → at most 0.2) and an accent color distinct from the water. New feature F031, after F021.
- Rejected or kept as is: the mouse "hesitation trap" (push.ts already takes the fastest 50 ms of the push); the "layering error" in bottleView (water drawn over a translucent shell is correct in 2D); 1/3/6 scoring and streak multipliers (the human chose 1/2/3 on 2026-11-16; streak bonuses stay a later idea); required triples (optional per the design); decoupling the bottle's look from its physics shape.
- Plan: F020 rewritten (new targets, calibration test ranges to update, depends on F030); F030 and F031 added; M5 goal updated. Order: F017 (fix), F030, F020, F021, F031, F022.

## Human session · independent Gemini review of M3 (2026-11-19)
- What: a second-opinion review of M3 (F013 to F015, commits `9351813..7c2a133`, judged in the current code) by Gemini 3.8 Flash (high) in Google Antigravity, run by hand outside the harness and read-only. It ran 9 unit test files one by one (all green), read the e2e tests and took 4 snaps (HUD at the start, a 1-throw round, the summary with "New best!", Play again by Space). Verdict: pass with issues, all 13 criteria met. Claude then checked each finding against the code.
- Findings and decisions:
  1. A save that can be read but not written loses the best score between rounds: **fix, new F032.** `SaveStore.load()` uses `lastSaved` only when there is no storage or `getItem` throws (`src/game/storage.ts:64-71`). With storage that is full or write-blocked, `setItem` throws but `getItem` works, so the next `load()` returns the old entry and ignores what was just recorded: the best falls back, later rounds show "New best!" again and the Title shows the old best. The mute choice itself stays right for the visit (it lives in `Sfx`), but a mute toggle also writes `lastSaved` from the stale best. The unit test's `throwingStorage` throws on both calls, so it never covered this. Rare in practice (a full quota on this origin, or old Safari private mode), but cheap to fix in `SaveStore` alone.
  2. `updateBest` accepts a fractional score, which `parseSave` would drop on reload: **no fix.** A score is a sum of whole turns (`Math.round` in the landing judge, `pointsForTurns` is `Math.abs`), and `recordRound` is only called with `snapshot.score`, so no fraction can reach it.
  3. The 10-throw script test in `roundPlay.test.ts` doesn't assert `roundOver` after its last result: **no fix.** The next test in the same file, and the "end of a round" block (`roundOver` after exactly the 10th wait, `outcome` null, no new bottle, every throw refused), already check that ending in detail.
- Plan: F032 added to M5 after F020 (depends on F015 and F017).
- Next: F020 attempt 2 (session 49 timed out with verify failing), then F032, F021, F031, F022.

## Human session · independent Gemini review of M2 (2026-11-19)
- What: a second-opinion review of M2 (F011 and F012, commits `940bc3b..3c89043`, judged in the current code) by Gemini 3.8 Flash (high) in Google Antigravity, run by hand outside the harness and read-only. It ran 5 unit test files one by one (all green), read the e2e tests, took snaps of a drag at 1280×720 and 1024×576 (power 14.0 and 13.5 for the same drag, so the push is measured in logical pixels), and probed `pushPower` at 30 Hz. Verdict: pass with issues, all 9 criteria met. Claude then checked each finding against the code with its own probes.
- Findings and decisions:
  1. The same push reads high at low frame rates: **fix, new F033.** Confirmed: a steady 200 px push over 150 ms gives 66.7 at 60 Hz and 1000 Hz but 77.8 at 30 Hz (120 px in 80 ms: +12.5; 40 px in 150 ms: +2.2), and one 50 ms hitch inside a 60 Hz push adds about 16. The cause is bigger than the constant Gemini named: browsers deliver one move per frame and Phaser listens to `mousemove` (no `getCoalescedEvents`), so the samples come at the frame rate, and `pushPower` reads every gap over `MAX_BRIDGE_SECONDS` (1/60 s, `src/core/push.ts:39`) as a pause followed by a burst. Gemini's suggested fix (a longer bridge) fixes steady 30 fps (1/30 s: within 1.9 points) but not a hitch (still +16, and +10.5 at 50 ms), and it makes the existing "flick after a short pause" case miss its 1-point bound (97.2 against 100), so F033 forwards the coalesced samples in src/game instead and leaves the rule alone.
  2. A push that goes up hard and comes back almost all the way down before the release still throws at about half power: **no fix.** It reproduces (250 px up, then 249 px down in the last 80 ms: 50.2), but it comes from the decided rule: speed is the fastest 50 ms of the window so a push that stops or eases off before the release keeps its throw (D21, and GAME_DESIGN "Not changed": "it already uses the fastest 50 ms of the push"). Zeroing speed when the pointer is not moving up at the release would undo that decision and also punish an ordinary follow-through (250 px up then 40 px down gives 85 today). A pointer that ends no higher than the window began already throws nothing.
  3. The real-mouse e2e test compares the power with `pushPower` itself: **no fix.** That test checks the wiring (the game sees the same events, with the same times, as the page's own log) at a pace Playwright doesn't control, so it can't assert design ranges. The rule is pinned to design targets in push.test.ts (300 px gives at least 90, 40 px under 30, more distance or speed gives more), and the virtual-clock e2e tests in mouse.spec.ts also assert ranges (a 200 px flick is above 50, a fast push beats a slow one by more than 30, a hard one beats a gentle one by more than 30).
- Plan: F033 added to M5 before F022 (depends on F012).
- Next: F020 attempt 2, then F032, F021, F031, F033, F022.

## Session 50 · builder · F020 Tune difficulty against simulated players
- Did: attempt 2. Session 49 had built most of it and timed out before logging, so I took its WIP commit (`9e6f87c`), checked it, removed its scratch files and finished the records.
  - `src/core/difficulty.ts` (new): `runBenchmark({ seed, bottles, errorSigma, tuning, sweep })`. It draws 200 random bottles the way the game does, sweeps each distinct water level once through the real `Simulation` (`sweepPowers`), and lets a practiced player (aims at each bottle's own single-flip window center) and a new player (one fixed power, the average center) throw with a seeded normal error (σ 8, Box-Muller `normalSample`, each player on its own seed stream). It counts landings and points, and reports the best fixed power (no error) and the best-scoring fixed power. The player models are in the file's header comment. Documented as D33 and in the file table of `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`.
  - `scripts/flip-windows.ts`: after the window table it prints both landing rates, the best fixed power and its rate, the best-scoring fixed power, and each player's points per 10 throws. It still takes `name=value` overrides.
  - `src/core/tuning.ts`: the retuned defaults below. I did not use the 2026-11-18 suggestion (shellDensity 0.32 and so on): with the other levers left at F009's values its flight barely rises. I measured the highest point of the bottle at a window center: 0.02 m above the held bottle's top at every level, against 0.44 to 0.49 m with F009's tuning. Session 49 searched around it with a flight constraint, and its tuning keeps the throw visible (table below).
  - Cleanup: `git rm` of session 49's eight scratch scripts `scripts/_*.ts` (the search, flight and rise probes; they stay in the history in `9e6f87c`). The 36 `scripts/_*.log` files it left are ignored by git and untracked; I left them, since a glob delete was denied.
- Tests (none removed or weakened):
  - `tests/unit/calibration.test.ts` follows the design change of 2026-11-18 (section 3 and 13): the ratio test is now 2.5 to 4.0 (was 1.5 to 2.0), the width test 10 to 28 (was 12 to 28), the tested levels are 10% to 90% in 10% steps (were 5 levels), and there are two new tests: no power from 0 to 100 lands a single flip on more than 60% of the nine levels, and every double-flip window starts below power 100.
  - New `tests/unit/difficulty.test.ts` (made-up worlds for the models, no physics; the benchmark of the default tuning is deterministic for a seed, practiced 60 to 80% and new 20 to 40% of 200 throws, the best fixed power at most about 60%) and an extended `tests/unit/flipWindowsScript.test.ts` (the script prints both rates, the best fixed power and its rate, and the points).
  - Older tests that threw at a fixed power expecting an outcome: only `tests/unit/soundPlan.test.ts` ("gives a whoosh, at least one knock and then a thud for a failed throw") needed a change. It threw at 20, which now lands upright for a half-full bottle; it now takes the power from a sweep (10 below the 50% window's low edge) and asserts the outcome is a fail. No e2e test had to change: all 228 pass with the new defaults.
- Tuning, old (F009) → new: `shellDensity` 0.9 → 0.2969, `throwLinearImpulse` 9.2 → 5.226, `throwAngularImpulse` 6.25 → 3.3933, `throwSpinGrowth` 0.013 → 0.0149, `throwSpinExponent` 0.03 → 0.0307, `bottleAngularDamping` 2.55 → 2.535, `bottleFriction` 0.7 → 0.7571, `bottleRestitution` 0.04 → 0.0182, `bottleLinearDamping` 0.34 → 0.3013, `throwAngleDeg` 6 → 5.893. Everything else is unchanged. Rounding them to three digits narrowed the 10% window to 9, so keep them as they are.
- New `npx tsx scripts/flip-windows.ts` (default tuning, 200 bottles, seed 1, σ 8):

```
       single flip (1 turn)         double flip (2 turns)
 water   low  high  center  width     low  high  center  width
   10%    11    21      16     10      47    54    50.5      7

   40%    28    43    35.5     15      70    79    74.5      9
   50%    32    47    39.5     15      75    84    79.5      9
   60%    37    52    44.5     15      80    88      84      8
   70%    41    57      49     16      85    93      89      8
   80%    46    62      54     16      90    99    94.5      9
   90%    52    67    59.5     15      96   100      98      4

player                                                      landing rate  points per 10
practiced player (aims at each window center)                      65.0%            6.5
new player (one fixed power, 38.5)                                 36.0%            3.8
best fixed power (54, no error)                                    60.5%            8.7
best-scoring fixed power (95, no error)                            55.5%           16.3
```

  The old tuning (F009) had single windows 13 to 16 wide with centers 29.5 (10%) to 54 (90%), a ratio of 1.8, and one fixed power (46) landing 7 of the 9 levels (the 2026-11-18 brief). Now the ratio is 3.7 (59.5 / 16), the 10% window (11 to 21) and the 90% window (52 to 67) are 31 points apart, and the most any fixed power lands a single flip is 4 of the 9 levels (for example 37, 41 or 46).
- Verified:
  - `npm run format`, then `npm run verify`: format check, typecheck, unit tests (729 in 55 files), build, and 228 e2e passed in 13.4 min. The harness had marked session 49's verify as failed, with `muteSummary.spec.ts:69` (the speaker click on the summary) at 40 s in the tail; it passes in this run and that test doesn't touch the tuning, so I take it for a slow run under load, but I have no log of the cause.
  - Snaps (`?seed=7`, 1280x720, `scene:Game,pause,call:lockWater;<w>,call:setPower;<center>,key:Space,step:...`): at the 10% (power 16), 50% (40) and 90% (60) window centers each bottle leaves the hand, turns over once (upside down at the top of the throw, the water line moving to the top) and stands upright on the table with the green flash, "Nailed it! +1" and Score 1. No console errors. Honest measure of the flight: the base of the bottle rises 1.15, 1.19 and 1.32 m above the hand (F009: 1.33, 1.31, 1.35 m), and the highest point of the bottle rises 0.24, 0.36 and 0.48 m above the top of the held bottle (F009: 0.44 to 0.49). The landing spot is the same as before, about 20 to 40 px right of the hand over the left half of the table (x 2.99 to 3.11 m against 3.03 to 3.10 m in F009).
- Status: F020 → needs_review
- Issues / follow-ups:
  - **The 10% bottle's flight is lower than F009's** (0.24 m above the held top against 0.44 m): at its apex the bottle still sits about where it was held. Raising `throwLinearImpulse` to 6.2 lifts it to 0.33 m but narrows that window to 8, under the design's 10. A higher flight at 10% needs a different lever or a taller toss designed in; the reviewer should look at the 10% snap and judge.
  - **Landing rates have little margin.** The default seed gives practiced 65.0% (target 60 to 80), new 36.0% (20 to 40) and a best fixed power of 60.5% on the benchmark's 200 bottles (the 60% target is tested on the nine levels, where the worst fixed power lands 4 of 9 single flips). One seed's 200 throws scatter by about ±3.5 points; the exact expected rates over 17 levels are practiced 65.7% and new 39.1% (D33). The 10% window is exactly 10 wide, the minimum, and the 90% double window is 4 wide (96 to 100).
  - **Multi-flips are not targeted.** A fixed power of about 95 with no error scores 16.3 points per 10 throws against the practiced player's 6.5, because high powers land double and triple flips at several levels. The design counts landings, so this is not a failure of F020's targets, but a human should decide whether to cap the points of one throw or remove the windows at high power. It is in the benchmark as `bestScoringFixed`.
  - The error size (σ 8) and the aiming rules are assumptions to confirm by playing, as the feature notes say.
  - Process: a glob in `rm` and a `for` loop with a shell variable were auto-denied; plain commands with exact paths worked.
- Next: the reviewer should play at the 10%, 50% and 90% window centers (`call:lockWater;<w>,call:setPower;<center>`), judge the 10% flight, and confirm `npx tsx scripts/flip-windows.ts` prints the table above. F032 is next after the review.

## Session 52 · builder · F032 Keep the best score and mute for the visit when the save can be read but not written
- Did: `SaveStore` in `src/game/storage.ts` now also remembers that its last `setItem` threw (`writeFailed`, set in `update`, cleared by the next write that works). While it is set, `load()` gives what the store last wrote (`lastSaved`), except that the best is the higher of that and the stored best (another tab's progress is kept). With working storage `load()` still reads the entry afresh every time, and a missing storage or a throwing `getItem` still gives `lastSaved` as before. `update` merges on top of `load()`, so the first write that works stores the in-memory values (best 8 survives). No change to `src/core` (`updateBest` is untouched). The storage row and D28 in `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` describe the new behavior.
  - Tests (none removed or changed): 16 new unit cases in `tests/unit/saveStore.test.ts` (a `readOnlyStorage` helper whose writes throw until a flag is set): for an empty storage and one holding a best of 3, `recordRound(8)` is a new best, `load().best` is 8, `recordRound(8)` and `recordRound(5)` are not, `update({ muted: true })` keeps the 8 and holds the mute, and a mute change before any round holds; the storage entry itself is not touched; a better best written by another tab wins (20 over 8); once writing works the next `update` stores best 8 and mute, then a value another tab writes (even a lower best, another mute) is read as it is; a second failure goes back to the fallback; a new store starts from the storage; a storage that can be written but not read never throws. Two new e2e tests in `tests/e2e/bestScore.spec.ts` (`Storage.prototype.setItem` throws from an init script): a 3 round shows "Best: 3" and "New best!", the next round of 2 shows "Best: 3" with no "New best!", the Title shows "Best: 3", nothing is stored and there are no console errors; and a saved best of 3 beaten by a round of 8 (new best), then 8 again and 1 (not), Title "Best: 8".
- Verified:
  - `npm run format`, then `npm run verify`: format check, typecheck, unit tests, build and 230 e2e passed in 14.1 min (228 before, +2). The "exited with code 0" in the background output is from `| tail`, but the e2e summary says 230 passed and the chain stops at the first failure.
  - With `src/game/storage.ts` stashed (the old code, tests kept): 11 of the 46 `saveStore.test.ts` cases fail, and both new e2e tests fail; the fix restored, they pass. The 30 older cases pass in both.
  - Snap of the Title (1280x720, `?seed=7`) and a start into the Game scene: Title shows "Best: 0", Play, the speaker and the hint, nothing overlapping or cut off; no console errors. The snap tool can't make `setItem` throw before the page loads, and this feature changes no drawing, so the e2e tests (which assert on the shown texts) are the evidence for the visible behavior.
- Status: F032 → needs_review
- Issues / follow-ups:
  - While writes fail, the mute choice comes from `lastSaved` and not from the stored entry, so a mute change made in another tab is not seen until a write works again. The assignment only asks to keep the best against another tab, so I left it.
  - A round that is not a new best doesn't write, so after a failed write the store only retries at the next `update` (a new best or a mute toggle). That is enough for the rule "until a write succeeds again" and avoids a write on every round.
  - A `Mute` toggle through `src/game/audio/mute.ts` already goes through `SaveStore.update`, so it gets the same fix; I didn't add an e2e test for the mute with `setItem` blocked (the unit tests cover it).
  - Process: a stash of one file (`git stash push -- <file>`, then `git stash pop`) worked to check that the new tests fail on the old code.
- Next: F021 is the next todo in M5 (see `feature_list.json`); the reviewer of F032 could also try `Storage.prototype.setItem = () => { throw ... }` in a real page with `?seed=7` through two rounds and the Title.

- Did: the three small effects, all in `src/game` and all pure functions of the simulation's ticks (D34), so a paused or stepped game shows an exact frame of them and the same events always give the same picture.

  - `GameScene` drains the events once per frame and feeds both the sounds and the effects; it calls `effectsView.stop()` before it pauses for the pause overlay or the summary (a paused scene's camera would otherwise hold a shake's offset).
  - Tuning: `shakeMinSpeed` 1, `shakeFullSpeed` 8, `shakeMaxPixels` 6 (the cap) and `shakeSeconds` 0.25 in `src/core/tuning.ts`, next to the knock's. They show up in the dev panel by themselves.
  - Message pop-in: `popScale(resultSteps)` in `resultStyle.ts` (0.5 at the outcome, about 1.12 at the peak, exactly 1 after 0.25 s), applied to the message's scale in `resultView.ts` by `resultSteps`, like the flash.

## Human session · Gemini check of the bottle's proportions (2026-11-19)
- What: the human noticed that the bottle has looked squeezed from top and bottom since M1 and asked Gemini 3.8 Flash (high) in Google Antigravity (read-only, conversation 7fa5641e) to check it. Claude checked its report against git, the docs, a snap and a probe of the flip windows.
- Findings and decisions:
  1. The bottle was made squat in F009 (commit `5dbf018`): **confirmed, fix, new F034.** Body 0.66 → 1.0 m wide, total height 1.74 → 1.13 m, neck 0.18 → 0.06 m tall; height to width 2.64:1 → 1.13:1 (a 500 ml bottle is about 3:1). It was a deliberate calibration lever (D23: a wide, low bottle tips back from about 0.27 of a turn instead of 0.1), and the 2026-11-18 design note kept it ("the bottle's shape stays as calibrated"), but on screen it reads as a jar or a water-cooler jug. That note is reversed in GAME_DESIGN.md (sections 3 and 13).
  2. Gemini's first remedy, drawing a slim bottle over the wide physics shape: **rejected.** The bottle would visibly stand back up from angles at which its drawn outline should fall, and the physics and the drawing are one shape on purpose (`bottleShape.ts`).
  3. Gemini's second remedy, retune the physics for a slimmer bottle: **taken, with a limit.** A probe with today's tuning (not retuned, shape restored afterwards): at 0.80 × 1.48 m (1.85:1) the single-flip windows stay 10 to 13 wide, but the ratio drops to 2.2, the 80% and 90% bottles lose their doubles and the practiced player lands 59%, so a retune is needed and looks within reach. The F002 shape (2.64:1) gives windows only 3 to 4 wide, so F034 asks for at least 1.8:1 (aim 2:1) and keeps every target; F025's notes ask whether sloshing leaves room to go slimmer.
  4. Gemini's claim that later milestones require a realistic bottle look for sloshing: **not found** in the design or the plan (but see 1 and section 3 now).
- Plan: F034 added to M5 after F021 (depends on F020 and F021); F031 now also depends on F034, so the shading is drawn for the final shape.
- Next: review F021, then F034, F031, F033, F022.
- Decision (later the same day, Claude at the human's request): the bottle gets about 2:1 now (F034), then as slim as sloshing allows, up to about 2.5:1 (new F035 in M6 after F025). Rejected: the original 2.64:1 now with narrower windows (windows 3 to 4 points wide, the practiced player at about 36% against 60 to 80%), and waiting for M6 (the jar look would stay through M5 and F031's shading would be drawn for a shape about to change). GAME_DESIGN section 3 states the goal and that the difficulty targets come first. F035 keeps F034's shape if no slimmer one meets every target, so it can always finish.

## Session 60 · builder · F033 Measure the mouse push from every pointer sample, not one per frame
- Did: while the bottle is grabbed the game now reads the canvas's native `pointermove` as well as Phaser's, and forwards each of its coalesced events to `sim.mouse.drag` with the event's own `timeStamp` and its height converted the way Phaser converts a pointer (`scene.scale.transformY(pageY)`). `pushPower` (push.ts) and the D25 timing rules are untouched, so every `push.test.ts` case passes unchanged.
  - `src/game/coalescedMoves.ts` (new, no Phaser, no DOM types): `CoalescedMoves` with `begin(pressMs)` at a grab, `fresh(pointermove)` (the coalesced samples newer than the press and than every sample already forwarded, in order; none for a touch or where `getCoalescedEvents` is missing or lists nothing; samples with no usable time or height are skipped) and `covers(ms)` (true when a forwarded sample already stands for that moment).
  - `src/game/gameInput.ts`: the native listener (gated on `sim.mouse.dragging`, removed on scene shutdown) and `covers` in Phaser's `pointermove` handler, so the same move is never forwarded twice; `begin` is called after a successful grab. The release still comes from Phaser's `pointerup` / `pointerupoutside`. Where there are no coalesced samples, `covers` is false and every Phaser move goes through exactly as before.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/coalescedMoves.test.ts` (12 cases) and `tests/e2e/mouseCoalesced.spec.ts` (7 tests), with helpers `mousePath`, `frameTimes` and `sendFrames` in `tests/e2e/mouseHelpers.ts` (a `pointermove` with `coalescedEvents` plus its compatibility `mousemove` per frame, on the virtual clock). Docs: D21, D25, the `gameInput.ts` row, new rows for `coalescedMoves.ts` and the two test files, and the `mouseHelpers.ts` row in `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`.
- Verified:
  - Criterion 1: a 200 px push over 150 ms at 30 fps with coalesced 125 Hz samples throws 66.67, the same push as plain 125 Hz events 66.83 (0.17 apart; the test allows 2). The same 30 fps push without the samples throws 75.83 (+9), so the coalesced events do reach the game (Chromium's `PointerEvent` keeps the `coalescedEvents` list and my shadowed `timeStamp`s).
  - Criterion 2: 60 fps with one 50 ms hitch carrying the missing samples throws 66.67, the push without the hitch 66.71; without the samples the hitch gives 82.72 (+16, as the review of M2 found).
  - Criterion 3: a push with plain events (empty `coalescedEvents`) throws `pushPower` of the page's mouse log to 6 digits; `mouse.spec.ts` and `mouseNothing.spec.ts` pass unchanged (22 tests, including "the same with the real mouse", where Chromium's own coalesced events and compatibility `mousemove` carry the same time stamps). Another test sends a `mousemove` at the same time stamp as the newest forwarded sample but 100 px higher: the bar doesn't move; one millisecond later it does.
  - Criterion 4: with the bottle not grabbed (aiming with no press and with a press away from it, in flight, during a result) coalesced moves leave the whole snapshot equal (power, `throws`, phase), and no console errors appear in any test.
  - Mutation check: with the native listener disabled the tests for criteria 1 and 2 and the repeat test fail with the numbers above, so they bite.
  - `npm run format`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run verify` passed (62 unit files, 830 passed, 1 todo: the F034 one; build; 248 e2e in 14.3 min). `npm run snap` from the Game scene (`mousedown`, `mousemove`, shot, `mouseup`, step): the live bar showed 27% mid-push, the thrown bottle left the hand and tumbled, no console errors, nothing misplaced.
- Status: F033 → needs_review
- Issues / follow-ups:
  - The feature_list.json left invalid after session 59 was already repaired by the harness; I checked it parses before and after my edit.
  - The e2e helpers fake the browser's `pointermove` + `mousemove` pair. Real Chromium's pair has equal time stamps (the unchanged real-mouse test passes to 6 digits). A browser that stamps the compatibility `mousemove` later than the coalesced list's last sample would forward one extra sample with the same height, which `pushPower` ignores (it joins equal heights without effect), so it can't change a power.
  - A touch is left to Phaser's own touch events on purpose (`pointerType === 'touch'`); the game is desktop-only. An event with an empty `pointerType` (script-made) counts as a mouse.
  - Process: a `python3 - <<EOF || node -e` compound Bash command was auto-denied; the Edit tool did the same job. The Monitor tool (`until grep -q ...; do sleep 30; done`) worked for waiting on the 14-minute `npm run verify`, but each watch expires after 10 minutes, so re-arm it.
- Next: F034 stays blocked for a human decision; of the todo features, pick the next one in `feature_list.json` whose dependencies are done (F031 shading must keep the bottle overscan and draw the final shape).

- Verdict: fail

Fix the tests so that every event of a push, the release included, takes its time from the virtual clock only (dispatch the mouseup in the same evaluate as the frames, or push `__virtualNow` ahead of real time before the press), then prove it with `--repeat-each 10`. - Did: new `src/core/slosh.ts` (`WaterSlosh`: the water as a second dynamic body, a mass-only sensor, on a `PrismaticJoint` along the shell's axis toward the cap, limited to 0 and `sloshLimit(level)` = the empty body height above the water; a spring and damper applied as forces before each world step; `summarize` and `setRigidVelocity` so the launch and `placeBottle` move both bodies as one rigid body with the solid model's mass, center and inertia). `bottle.ts` builds it instead of the water fixture when the switch is on, `simulation.ts` calls `beforeStep()`, `BottleSnapshot` gains `waterOffset` (always 0 when solid). Tuning gets `sloshing` (a number, 0 off by default, so the panel and every `Tuning` consumer stay numeric), `sloshFrequency` 3 and `sloshDamping` 0.5. `scripts/flip-windows.ts` takes `--sloshing` (or `sloshing=1`) and prints a first line saying so. Tests: `tests/unit/slosh.test.ts` (10), one more in `flipWindowsScript.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/slosh.spec.ts` (2). ARCHITECTURE.md has a row for slosh.ts.

  water   single low high center width   double low high center width
    10%         6   15   10.5     9           64   69   66.5     5
    20%         9   20   14.5    11           76   88   82      12
    30%        10   23   16.5    13           79  100   89.5    21
    40%        11   24   17.5    13           78   94   86      16
    50%        11   24   17.5    13           67   81   74      14

  So with the current throw numbers sloshing breaks the difficulty targets: the single windows collapse to power 6 to 26 for every level (one fixed power lands everything). Retuning is for a later feature.
- Status: F023 → needs_review
- Issues / follow-ups:
  - Physics finding: the water is always below the bottle's center of mass (the light shell's mass is high up), so a spin pins it onto the base. It slides toward the cap mostly in the landing (a stop, a tip, a bounce), where it changes the balance, not in the air. In the air it moves little.
  - The joint solver overshoots a limit by up to a step of travel on a hard stop (offset about -0.08 on a fast landing), so `WaterSlosh.offset` clamps to [0, limit]; the spring uses the raw value. A bottle resting on its cap keeps the water at about 0.028 m (gravity against the spring), not 0.
  - The drawing doesn't show the water's offset yet.
  - Process: a heredoc in a `cat > file` command, a `( time ... )` group and `tee` were auto-denied; Write, Edit and `node -e` worked. The `sloshing` switch is a number, not a boolean, to keep the tuning panel and store numeric.
- Next: F035 and the later M6 features should recalibrate with `--sloshing` (the table above is the uncalibrated start); if the toward-the-cap motion in the air is wanted, the joint axis or the spin-up impulse (applied to the shell only) is the lever.

If the design wants the water flung toward the cap mid-air (the description's "spinning flings it toward the cap"), a later feature needs a different lever.

Upright at rest it sits at -0.005 (linear slop). `WaterSlosh.offset` clamps these values, so the reported offset is always within limits, but the physical water is briefly outside them. A stiffer limit (more position iterations, or a speed cap on the water) would fix the real cause if it matters for calibration.
  - The water body gets no linear or angular damping, so with sloshing on the bottle as a whole spins down more slowly than the solid one (the shell's strong `bottleAngularDamping` acts only on the shell). That may explain part of the collapsed single windows in the `--sloshing` table, so it's worth checking when recalibrating.

## Human session · 2026-11-22 · Rule change: leaving the top of the screen and landing on the cap (human decisions)
- Leaving the top of the screen: the human decided it is not a fail; only the landing counts. outcome.ts already ignored the top edge, so no code change; GAME_DESIGN.md section 2 now says so, and tests/unit/offscreen.test.ts locks it (a 10% double flip that is fully above the screen for a moment lands as a success, two turns).

It scores the full turns completed in the air (`Math.trunc` of the rotation, since a bottle on its cap has turned k and a half), at least 1, with the message "Cap landing! +N" and the green flash. `flipWindow.findWindows` counts upright landings only, so the windows don't change. Measured: 50 of 1717 throws (17 levels x 101 powers) end on the cap, half at power 1 to 15 (a weak push tips the bottle half a turn).
- Rates after the change: windows unchanged; expected practiced 61.0% (was 60.9%), new player 44.7% (was 44.1%, limit 45%: the margin is now 0.3 points); seed 1 practiced 63.0%, new 38.5%; expected points 6.10 against the best fixed aim's 5.66.
- Tests changed because the rule changed (not weakened): landingJudging.test.ts (on the cap: success, 0 turns, 1 point), outcome.test.ts (on the cap: success), sweepPowers.test.ts (a cap success scores max(1, turns)), e2e flipRule.spec.ts (a real throw that ends on the cap follows the cap rule; 'Cap landing!' added to the known messages). New: resultStyle.test.ts cap message, flipRule.spec.ts cap landing with 0 and 2 credited turns.
- Docs: GAME_DESIGN.md sections 2 and the 2026-11-22 decisions (written by Claude in a human session at the human's request), ARCHITECTURE.md outcome row.

## Human session · independent Copilot review of M1 and M5 (2026-11-23)
- What: a second-opinion review of M1 (F001 to F010) and M5 (F030, F020, F032, F021, F034, F031, F033, F022) by GitHub Copilot (Copilot Fusion, gpt-6-luna), run by hand outside the harness. It was told the design in the prompt, not allowed to read harness/, and given 29 source and test files; it made 58 read-only tool calls and no edits. Claude then checked each finding against the code with its own sweeps.
- Findings and decisions:
  1. A tuning change during a result changes that result: **fix, new F036.** Confirmed: `Simulation.resultLength()` (`src/core/simulation.ts:264`) reads `resultDelaySeconds` and `replaySpeed` from the live tuning on every result step, so the tuning panel (or `setTuningValue`) shortens or lengthens the result on screen, against D9 and F010. The landing judge is fine: it keeps the tuning object it got at the throw. Dev builds and `?debug` only, so minor.
  2. The calibration tests skip half the water levels: **fix, added to F025.** Confirmed: `calibration.test.ts` sweeps 10%, 20% ... 90%, while the game draws 17 levels in 5% steps. A sweep of all 17 with the default tuning finds one target missed: at 15% water a miss at power 68 splits the double-flip window into 65 to 67 and 69 to 71, each 2 wide, against the 4-point minimum. Every other level passes (singles 11 to 15 wide, centers rising, doubles 5 to 8 wide), and the fixed-power rule still holds over 17 levels (power 35 lands 10 of 17, 58.8%). F025 retunes everything with sloshing on, so a retune now would be thrown away; F025 now first extends the calibration test to every drawn level, and F035 inherits it.
- Plan: F036 added to M5 after F022 (depends on F010, F030, F027). F025's description and second criterion now require the 17-level calibration test. M5 goal updated.
- Next: F036, then M6 continues (F024, F025, F035). The Gemini review of M1 and M5 is still to come.

## Human session · independent Gemini review of M1 and M5 (2026-11-23)
- What: a second-opinion review of M1 and M5 by Gemini 3.8 Flash (high) in Google Antigravity (conversation d1585c63), run by hand outside the harness and read-only. It ran 29 unit test files one by one (all green), ran scripts/flip-windows.ts and took 8 snaps. Verdict: M1 pass, M5 pass with issues. Claude then checked each finding against the code. Its criteria checklist is not usable: it lists criteria and code that don't exist (Space held to charge, a 10° limit, 10 water levels from 5% to 95%, `RESULT_HOLD_S`, a 1.2 m table, an apex within 5%), so it never read the real acceptance criteria. It did not find either finding of the Copilot review above.
- Findings and decisions:
  1. The three-rounds test in awkward.spec.ts asserts `score <= 10`, a stale bound under turn scoring (a double scores 2): **fixed in this session.** It could not fail with this test's throws, but it proved little, and F022's second criterion said the same. The test now keeps the sum of each throw's `outcome.points` in the round and asserts the score equals it after every throw; F022's criterion is reworded to match. That is a stronger check, not a weaker one.
  2. `CoalescedMoves.fresh` drops a coalesced sample older than one already forwarded: **no fix.** It is deliberate (`pushPower` needs samples in time order, and the doc comment says so), and the Pointer Events spec delivers the coalesced list in time order; sorting would mix late samples into a push already measured.
  3. `src/core/simulation.ts` is over the ~300-line guideline: **no fix here.** It is 340 lines, not 312 as reported, and the helpers Gemini named to extract (`isSettled`, `isRestingOnBody`, `contactNormal`) don't exist. It grew with M6 replay work; splitting it is the maintainer's routine cleanup, not an M5 defect.
- Also found while checking finding 1: the same three-rounds test was flaky before this change (the unchanged test failed 1 of 3 runs at a real-mouse push, `throws` 25 instead of 26). Playwright's mouse takes as long as the browser takes to answer, so on a busy machine the release came after the 150 ms push window had emptied and nothing was thrown. The push now goes through `dragInPage` on the virtual clock (40 px in 60 ms), like the other mouse tests. Nothing is checked less: the push is still mouse events through Phaser, and the test still waits for the throw.

## Session 87 · planner (extend)
- Did: added nine M7 "Phones" features, F037 to F045, covering design sections 7 and 10 and the M7 line of section 11. F037: a `phone` Playwright project (Chromium, 740×360 landscape, isMobile, hasTouch, tests/e2e/phone/ only), touch helpers on a virtual clock, and a finger push that throws through the mouse's grab with source `touch`. F038: `npm run snap -- --phone` / `--portrait` and the tap and touch actions. F039: every button and the skip work by tap, exactly once (no ghost mouse events), the sound unlocks on the first tap, a second finger and touchcancel are handled. F040: no scroll, zoom, selection or long-press menu, `100dvh`, safe-area padding, fullscreen and landscape lock on the Play tap where allowed. F041: the "Turn your phone sideways" overlay, which pauses a round under way. F042: thumb-sized hit areas (at least 44 CSS px on the phone) and a buttons hook. F043: text at least 12 CSS px on the phone, and finger hints instead of key hints on touch devices. F044: `npm run dev:phone` / `preview:phone`, a touch button for the tuning panel with ?debug, and README's "On a phone". F045: the finger push's own tuning numbers, set by mm of travel on the reference phone. F037 depends on F035 and F036, so no phone work starts before M6 (and F036) is done. No new milestone. Added a note to F028 (notes are the only field the planner may change). Rewrote the Plan section of docs/ARCHITECTURE.md (M7 modules, `touch` in the snapshot, P12 to P14), back under 60 lines.
- Milestones:
  - M7 Phones: F037–F045, from the phone test project and the finger push to taps, page behavior, the portrait message, button and text sizes, opening the game on a real phone and the finger push's tuning; the human checks a real Android phone and iPhone at its stop.
  - M8 Release readiness: unchanged (F029 done, F028 todo; see the conflict below).
- Conflict, for the human: F028 (M8, performance) sits before F037–F045 in the list and depends only on F025 and F027, so the harness will build it right after F035, before M7, although its third criterion needs F037's phone project. The planner can't reorder features or change depends_on (the harness reverts it). Please move F028 after F045 and add F045 to its depends_on, as planned in the human session above. Until then, F028's note tells a builder that reaches it early to set it blocked rather than build the phone setup itself.
- Assumptions:
  - The emulated phone is 740×360 CSS px (a 6-inch Android phone sideways, the smallest common landscape phone screen), where Scale.FIT draws the canvas at exactly half size. "Readable" is checked as text at least 12 CSS px tall (24 game px), and "big enough for a thumb" as a hit area of at least 44×44 CSS px (88 game px), the usual touch-target minimum.
  - The phone project runs only tests/e2e/phone/, not the whole desktop suite again: the gate is 45 minutes and the desktop suite already takes about 19.
  - A finger push uses the same grab, `pushPower` and `throwBottle` as the mouse, with throw source `touch` and its own tuning fields (`touchFullDistance`, `touchFullSpeed`, `touchMinPower`, `touchGrabPadding`). F045's targets are best guesses: full power at 30 to 60 mm of steady finger travel, nothing thrown under 2 mm of jitter, each single-flip window at least 3 mm of travel, at about 5.5 CSS px per mm. The difficulty targets hold on a phone by construction, because they are measured in power.
  - A "touch device" is one with no fine pointer (`(any-pointer: fine)` false). It gets the finger hints, the portrait message and the fullscreen request; a touchscreen laptop keeps the desktop behavior.
  - The portrait message is an HTML overlay, and turning upright during a round opens the existing pause overlay, so the game doesn't go on unseen.
  - "Fills the screen as much as the browser allows": `100dvh`, plus fullscreen and a landscape lock requested by the Play tap (Android Chrome), silently refused on iPhone Safari. The safe area is CSS padding from `env(safe-area-inset-*)`, read through custom properties so a test can simulate a notch.
  - The human opens the game on a phone through `npm run dev:phone` on the home network, not a hosted page (hosting stays "later"). No test starts a network server, because on Windows that raises a firewall prompt in an unattended run.
- Open questions:
  1. F028's position and dependency (above).
  2. Design section 13 says to revisit Playwright's Firefox at M7. The plan adds none; Firefox and Safari stay manual checks at milestone stops. Should a WebKit project emulating an iPhone run the phone specs too? It would catch some Safari touch differences, but it needs `npx playwright install webkit` and adds gate time.
  3. Are F045's finger-travel ranges (full power at 30 to 60 mm) about right? The human judges them on real phones at the M7 stop, with the tuning panel that F044 makes reachable by touch.

## Human session · 2026-11-23 · F025: root cause is the sloshing water's damping (replaces the spin-curve advice above)
- A second review found the real reason F025 could not be tuned. The water body in src/core/slosh.ts has no damping; only the shell gets bottleAngularDamping (5.01/s). The fuller the bottle, the more of its spin goes undamped: in-flight spin decay at power 35 is 4.8/s at every level with solid water, but 4.3/s (10%), 3.3/s (50%), 2.3/s (90%) with sloshing. So full bottles keep their spin and every level needs the same power: with sloshing on, the default tuning gives single windows of 12 to 19.5 at every level, and one fixed power (15) lands 100%. The water never moves in flight (offset 0.000), so this is a modeling bug, not slosh physics, and no spin curve (a function of power, the same for every bottle) can fix it.
- Check, scratch only and reverted: damping shell and water as one rigid body gave decay 4.9 to 5.2/s at every level and single windows 23.5 to 53 (ratio 2.3), widths 13 to 20; doubles moved above 100 and one fixed power landed 87.5%, so a normal retune is still needed.
- F025's notes now say: fix the damping first (own commit, unit test that the decay is the same at every level), then retune from there with scripts/retune-search.ts, all targets kept; change the spin curve only if that fails. Fallback unchanged: relax only the fixed-power landing limit.
- Next: builder F025.
