## Human session · 2026-11-24 · F025 done: the sloshing spin leak fixed, sloshing on by default, recalibrated
- Root cause of sessions 95-101 (and of the human sessions' damping theories): the water as a second body on a prismatic joint leaked angular momentum in planck at high spin. With every damping at 0 and the water never leaving the base, a power-80 throw at 50% kept 59% of its spin after 5 steps and 31% after 40 (solid water: 100%); more iterations changed nothing, 16 substeps still lost 20%. The leak grows with spin, so rotation stopped growing with power: no doubles, bunched centers, one fixed power landing 80%+. A split in-air/contact damping probe (scratch, not committed) gave doubles back but singles 5 to 11 wide (practiced 45 to 55%), because it was still fighting the leak.
- Fix (7b5dbd2, D35): a sloshing bottle keeps its water as the solid fixture through the throw and the flight, and `Bottle.beforeStep` releases it into the sliding body when `shouldReleaseWater` says the lowest point is near the tabletop (0.05 m plus two steps of fall). This is exact for the model: the water's center is nearer the base than the bottle's center of mass at every level, and in the old model the water first moved on the step of first table contact too. Tests: flight identical to solid until release, spin kept with no damping, release before the first contact at every level and power, release keeps the motion; damping.test.ts now wants the sloshing decay equal to solid's (it allowed 15%: the leak).
- Retune and default (8fe4a80): `sloshing` 0 -> 1. Old -> new: shellDensity 0.3353956 -> 0.3309721, throwLinearImpulse 6.7033068 -> 6.9259249, throwAngularImpulse 8.5868222 -> 8.7514641, throwSpinExponent 0.0342130 -> 0.0323858, throwSpinGrowth 0.0142491 -> 0.0153780, bottleAngularDamping 5.0115218 -> 5.0366878, bottleFriction 0.1411787 -> 0.1465280, bottleLinearDamping 0.2487038 -> 0.2400720, sloshFrequency 3 -> 3.0564694, sloshDamping 0.5 -> 0.5364722; bottleRestitution 0.0239780 and throwAngleDeg 13.9 unchanged. calibration.test.ts covers all 17 levels (derived from the tuning). The old solid tuning failed one 17-level check (15% double 2 wide); the leaky sloshing model failed 25.
- `npx tsx scripts/flip-windows.ts` (now 17 rows, `--solid` for solid water):

- Benchmark: seed 1 (200 bottles): practiced 62.5%, new 38.5% (aim 37.7), best fixed power 39 lands 56.5%, best-scoring fixed power 82 (no error) 37.0% / 7.4 points. A first run had two e2e failures: tests/e2e/mouse.spec.ts:47 (the known wall-clock test, passes alone) and my slosh.spec.ts landing throw at power 60, which was still rolling at step 150; it now uses 55. Test changes, all where a test stated the old default or a throw tied to the leaky physics: slosh.test.ts, slosh.spec.ts, tuningPanel.spec.ts (switch starts checked; a throw after unchecking shows no offset), flipWindowsScript.test.ts (17 rows, `--solid`; the default run is shared, 93 s -> 27 s).

## Human session · 2026-11-24 · F025 outside review (Copilot)
- Copilot reviewed 7b5dbd2..76cff75 (read-only, own probes). It reproduced the spin leak with the pre-change code (0.307 of the spin left after 40 steps, 0.804 with 16 substeps) and the expected rates (60.195% / 44.793%), and found no weakened test.
- Finding 1 (real, fixed in "fix(F025): release the pinned water before a fast-spinning corner touches"): the release trigger followed only the center's fall, so with little damping the water was still pinned at the first table contact (337 of 1,717 throws with no damping, 20 at angular damping 1; default tuning 0). `lowestPointAhead` now predicts the lowest point with the spin; 0 in every case, default tables and rates unchanged.
- Finding 2 (real, fixed): the release test now covers every power and also no damping.
- Finding 3 (rejected): moving `SLOSH_RELEASE_MARGIN` into tuning.ts. It is a numerical safety margin (outcomes don't depend on it as long as the release precedes contact, which is tested), like the solver iteration counts; as a tuning field it would show in the panel as a fake feel knob.
- Finding 4 (accepted as is, the human's decision): the water does not move in flight. In this model it can only slide along the axis and the spin presses it onto the base, as in a real throw; real water also spreads toward both ends in flight and slows the spin, which the game's strong in-air angular damping stands in for. A water model that spreads in flight would be a new feature with a new calibration.
- Verified: `npm run verify` green after the fix (format, typecheck, 899 unit, build, 273 e2e).
- Status: F025 stays needs_review.

- Finding 1 (the late release): the same as Copilot's, already fixed in 00ef25a.
- Finding 2 (risk, acknowledged): the expected rates sit about 0.2 points inside their limits, so one power point at a window edge flips difficulty.test.ts. Known (D35); F035's retune must score margins, not just pass.

- Its correction to claim 2 is right: the old joint model moved the water up to about 1 cm in the first two steps at power 80 to 100 (a solver transient; measured 1.05 cm at 50%, power 100). D35 corrected (27d6d53).

## Human session · 2026-11-24 · visible water, second pass (D36)
- Why: the human found the first pass unrealistic: the water sloshed in the air (it followed world gravity in a spinning bottle) and on the cap it stopped at the shoulder instead of running into the neck.

Driven by simulated ticks, not frame time (frames with 0 or 2 steps made the acceleration estimate jumpy), so it also slows with the replay. ## Human session · 2026-11-24 · replay water fix (D36)
- Why: the human saw the water swing round the bottle once at the start of every replay, never in the live throw. Cause: the replay opens 0.4 s before the landing, mid-flight; the water model restarted with zero speed, so the first replay frame read the flight speed as a huge push.
- Did: after a start, `WaterMotion` waits for a measured speed and then a measured acceleration (now smoothed itself, not derived from a smoothed speed) before the water moves. Headless check (seed 7, 50% water, power 75): live and replay both keep the water flat on the base until the landing and peak at -117 degrees in the slosh. New unit test: starting on a bottle already in free flight does not tip the water.

## Human session · 2026-11-24 · shadow follows the bottle's angle
- Why: the human found the shadow unrealistic: a bottle lying on its side still cast the short shadow of its base. With the light straight above, the shadow should cover the bottle as seen from above.
- Did: `bottleShadow.ts` now projects the bottle's outline onto the table at its current angle (`bottleExtent`): the shadow spans its leftmost to rightmost point plus a soft rim, centered under it, so it is as wide as the body upright and runs the whole length on its side. Its depth (drawn height) depends on the height only, not the angle. `bottleShadowView.ts` draws it from one gradient texture cut into two rounded ends and a stretched middle (`softTextures.ts`), so a long shadow stays dark along its length; the old single blob texture is gone. The hook `shadow()` also reports `x` and `depth`. Presentation only, no change in src/core.
- Tests: `softShading.test.ts` (upright and lying widths and centers, never shorter than upright at any angle, longer when tilted); `shadow.spec.ts` gets a side-landing test (seed 7, 50% water, power 75 lands on its side: shadow over 1.4 times the upright width, centered under the bottle). The old e2e test compared widths at rest and high in a flight; a tilted bottle in the air now has a longer footprint than an upright one, so it compares `depth` (the height-only size cue) instead, which is what it meant.
- Verified with `npm run snap` (`?seed=7`, also at 2560x1440): shadow under the diagonal bottle in flight, a long smooth shadow from base to cap on the side landing, no seams between the pieces.
- `npm run verify`: format, typecheck, unit, build green; e2e 273 of 274. The one failure, mouse.spec.ts:47 (real mouse at the machine's pace: the release never throws, phase stays `aiming`), fails the same with this change taken out, three times out of three alone, with `npm run dev` running alongside; not caused by the shadow, left for a look.

Desktop snap at 1024x576 (`?seed=7`, mouse push): the bar shows the live push (17%), the throw flies with source 'mouse', no console errors. The diff weakens no test; the only change to an existing test is the awkward.spec.ts race fix above.
- Problems: none
- Notes for later: (1) The doc comment of `bindGameInput` (src/game/gameInput.ts) still says "keyboard, wheel and mouse input" and "Left button down"; it could mention that a finger is the same grab. (2) The phone spec checks the live bar only once, at the end of the held push; my midway check is not in the suite. (3) A real-time CDP touch push gives a low power (10) because the samples arrive at the machine's pace; tests that need an exact power should keep using the virtual-clock helpers.

## Session 112 · reviewer · F039 Tap every button and skip the wait with a finger
- Verdict: pass
- Evidence: `npm run verify` green: format, typecheck, unit, build, e2e 286 of 286 (19.7 min), including tap.spec.ts 8 of 8 on the phone project and every desktop mouse and keyboard test unchanged (the test diff only adds tests/e2e/phone/tap.spec.ts). Code: one change in src/game/gameInput.ts (`pointer.wasCanceled` → `sim.mouse.cancel()`); I checked Phaser 3.90's source: `Pointer.touchcancel` sets `wasCanceled` and the InputPlugin then emits `pointerup` (upElement is the canvas), so the branch is the one taken. A scratch phone spec (deleted) with real touches passed: (1) the summary's Title tap lands on Title and it is still only Title 800 ms later (no stray Play); (2) the same after the pause overlay's Quit to title; (3) pause Restart at throw 2 gives throw 1, aiming, no grab (lastThrow survives a restart by design, `Simulation.restartRound`); (4) three rapid speaker taps toggle three times (muted true), nothing thrown; (5) a tap during the flight neither skips nor changes the throw; (6) a real CDP `touchCancel` mid-push leaves dragging false, nothing thrown, and a fresh push afterwards still throws with source touch; (7) the reverse of the builder's two-finger test: the first finger lifts while the second is still down → it throws (source touch), the second's later move and lift change nothing and the result is throw 1. Phone snap (`--phone`, taps only): Play, pause tap shows the overlay, Resume leaves throw 1 aiming with no grab, a finger push throws (source touch), a tap skips to throw 2 with lastThrow unchanged; no console errors. Desktop snap 1024x576: click Play, mouse push throws (source mouse), click skips to throw 2.
- Problems: none
- Notes for later: (1) A touchcancel while a finger is pressed on a button (src/game/view/buttonView.ts activates on any `pointerup` after its `pointerdown`) still activates it: a CDP touchStart on the speaker then touchCancel toggled the mute. Outside F039's criteria, but a system gesture over Quit or Restart would act; the button could ignore `pointer.wasCanceled`. (2) `pointerupoutside` in gameInput.ts doesn't check `wasCanceled`; for touches it never fires in practice (a touch's target stays the canvas). (3) CDP `Input.dispatchTouchEvent` lifts points by position in the list, not by id, so lifting the first of two fingers by CDP actually lifts the second; use identifier-addressed synthetic TouchEvents for that case.

## Session 114 · reviewer · F040 Keep the page still on a phone and fill the screen
- Verdict: fail
- Evidence: `npm run verify` green (format, typecheck, unit 920/920, build, e2e 292/292 in 18.9 min); page.spec.ts 4/4 (phone) and fullscreen.spec.ts 2/2 (desktop) also run on their own. `--phone` snaps: Title and Play fill the full 360 px height with letterbox only at the sides, no scrollbar or margin; a finger push throws; no console errors. Desktop snap 1024x576: click Play, mouse throw fine. Scratch phone specs (deleted): contextmenu dispatched by hand on the canvas and on the letterbox, and a right-click, were all default-prevented; a real (unstubbed) requestFullscreen on the Play tap gives no errors and the round runs; two quick Play taps request fullscreen once, Enter on the phone never does; window resizes 740x360 → 600x300 → 740x360 → 360x740 → 740x360 all come out right. Changing the insets beyond 50 px a side breaks (problem 1).
- Problems: (1) The ResizeObserver's bare `game.scale.refresh()` sizes the canvas from Phaser's stale cached parentSize and only then updates the cache, so the canvas lags one change behind and Phaser's 500 ms poll never corrects it. With insets 100/60 px the canvas stayed 640x360 at x 70 (30 px under the left inset); back at 0 it stayed 580x326.25. Fix: `if (game.scale.getParentBounds()) game.scale.refresh()`, or drop the observer. (2) page.spec.ts doesn't catch it: 44 px insets never force a shrink (652 ≥ 640); add a shrinking inset and the return to 0. (3) The long-press test passes without testing anything: a CDP long touch fires no contextmenu in headless Chromium (logged []), and `[].every(Boolean)` is true; dispatch cancelable contextmenu events on the canvas and the letterbox and assert defaultPrevented. Details in review_feedback.
- Notes for later: the #safe padding area outside #game (only non-zero on a notched phone) has no contextmenu guard; CSS callout none covers most of it. Setting `--safe-*` on documentElement from a Playwright init script doesn't stick (the parser replaces the html element's attributes), so test insets after load.

- Did: four touch fields in `src/core/tuning.ts` (`touchFullDistance`, `touchFullSpeed`, `touchMinPower`, `touchGrabPadding`; they show in the tuning panel like every key). `pushTuningFor(tuning, source)` in `src/core/mouseControl.ts` swaps them in for the full distance, full speed, minimum power and grab padding when the grab is a touch (`canGrab` takes an optional source); the mouse path gets the same object back. Tests: `tests/unit/touchTuning.test.ts` (12) and `tests/e2e/phone/fingerPush.spec.ts` (2 mm throws nothing; 60 mm over 150 ms throws power 100, source touch). `tests/e2e/phone/touch.spec.ts` expected the live power from the mouse numbers; it now uses the touch numbers (the same check, the right tuning).
- Old and new touch values: a touch used the mouse's numbers before, now its own. Full distance 300 px to 450 px (11 px per mm: 27 mm to 41 mm). Full speed 2000 to 3000 px/s. Minimum power 1 to 10. Grab padding 12 px to 48 px (1.1 mm to 4.4 mm). Mouse values unchanged (300, 2000, 1, 12).
- Results on the reference phone (steady push over 150 ms): power 100 first at about 41 mm; 2 mm gives about 4.9; no push of up to 2 mm at any speed passes about 9.8, under touchMinPower 10, so it throws nothing. Single-flip window centers (power, finger travel): 10% water 20, 8.2 mm; 15% 25.5, 10.4; 20% 29, 11.9; 25% 32, 13.1; 30% 33.5, 13.7; 35% 35.5, 14.5; 40% 36.5, 14.9; 45% 37.5, 15.3; 50% 38.5, 15.7; 55% 39.5, 16.2; 60% 40.5, 16.6; 65% 42.5, 17.4; 70% 43.5, 17.8; 75% 45.5, 18.6; 80% 47.5, 19.4; 85% 50, 20.5; 90% 52.5, 21.5 mm. Window widths are 12 to 14 points, which is 5 to 6 mm of travel.
- Verified: format, typecheck, new unit file, phone e2e (fingerPush, touch); full `npm run verify` result is the harness line below.
- Status: F045 → needs_review
- Issues / follow-ups: the whole range of a finger is 0 to 41 mm, so the common 8 to 22 mm window centers sit in the lower half of a flick; the human may want a smaller full distance on a real phone (tune it in the panel). The power curve is linear in travel, so a harder flick is not rewarded beyond full power.
- Next: reviewer: run full verify; check `touchTuning.test.ts` jitter test against other speeds if you doubt the 10 cap (it is distance/50 ms bound).

## Session 128 · builder · F045 Tune the finger push
- Did: found the real cause of the repeating `phone/tap.spec.ts` "a second finger…" failure. `sendTouch` starts each call at `max(virtualNow, performance.now())`, so the real delay between the held push (`end: 'hold'`) and the final `touchend` (a few hundred ms on a loaded machine) became a pause in the finger's motion, the release speed fell and the power went under touchMinPower 10: no throw, phase `aiming`. Session 127's longer push did not remove that. Now `sendTouch` has an option `{ chain: true }` (continue exactly from the previous call's last virtual time) and that test's final `touchend` uses it. No change to `src/` or the touch numbers. Old and new touch values and the single-flip travel list are in the session 126 entry (full distance 300→450, full speed 2000→3000, min power 1→10, grab padding 12→48; mouse unchanged).
- Verified: format, typecheck, phone e2e tap/touch/fingerPush (14 passed). The full `npm run verify` is the harness's run after this session.
- Status: F045 → needs_review
- Issues / follow-ups: other phone tests that hold a push across two `sendTouch` calls and then release (tap.spec touchcancel is fine, it throws nothing) could use `chain` the same way if they ever flake.
- Next: reviewer: run full verify with nothing else running; if thumb.spec flakes, raise its test timeout rather than touching the tuning.

## Session 129 · reviewer · F045 Tune the finger push
- Verdict: pass
- Evidence: `npm run verify` green: format, typecheck, unit 950/950, build, e2e 327/327 (29.7 min, nothing else running), including the two tests that failed the last gate (phone touch.spec.ts:29 and the thumb.spec Summary corner taps) and phone/fingerPush.spec.ts (2 mm throws nothing; 60 mm in 150 ms throws 100, source touch, on the virtual clock through the real page). Mutations in src/core/tuning.ts, each undone afterwards (tree checked clean): touchMinPower 1 + touchGrabPadding 12 fail the jitter and 4 mm padding tests in touchTuning.test.ts; touchFullDistance 300 + touchFullSpeed 3000→2000 fail the 30–60 mm test, the jitter test and the ≥ 3 mm window test. By hand from push.ts: 2 mm is 22 game px, (22/450 + (22/0.05)/3000)/2 = 9.8 power at most, so the 10 minimum holds at any speed, though only just. The single-flip windows are measured with the same sweepPowers/findWindows/widestWindow as calibration.test.ts. The tuning panel lists the keys from `Object.keys(DEFAULT_TUNING)`, so the four touch fields show up. gameInput.ts passes `pointer.wasTouch ? 'touch' : 'mouse'` to grab, and release/showPush use the grab's source. Snaps (no console errors): `--phone`, a 22 game px finger flick in 30 ms on the bottle leaves phase aiming, lastThrow null, bar back to 50; the same flick with a mouse at 1024x576 still throws (7.4, source mouse), so the mouse path is unchanged. A long finger flick and a mouse flick both throw and render fine (source touch / mouse). The progress entry of session 126 lists old→new touch values and each water level's single-flip center in mm. Test diff: touchThrow.test.ts and touch.spec.ts now expect the touch numbers (correct, a finger reads its own now). The mouse-equality test now sets the touch fields equal to the mouse's, so it still checks one conversion. tap.spec raised a 30 px push to 120 px, chained its release, and awkward/thumb timeouts went up. None of that weakens a check.
- Problems: none
- Notes for later: (1) Geometry on the reference phone: the bottle's middle is about 193 CSS px (35 mm) below the top of a 360 CSS px screen, and power 100 needs 450 game px (41 mm) of travel inside the 150 ms window. So full power is only reachable by grabbing the lower part of the bottle and flicking to the top edge, and from the middle a flick to the top edge gives about 86. Every single-flip center is at most 22 mm, so play isn't affected, but the human should keep this in mind when judging touchFullDistance at the M7 stop. The fingerPush e2e "60 mm" push ends about 137 CSS px above the top of the page, which only a synthetic event can do. (2) The jitter margin is thin: a 2.1 mm flick in under 50 ms would throw. (3) `npm run snap` moves in real time over CDP, which stretches pushes: the 386 px / 150 ms move gave 19 by finger and 36 by mouse (nominal 86 and 100). Use the virtual-clock e2e helpers for power numbers, not snap. (4) docs/ARCHITECTURE.md doesn't mention the touch fields or `pushTuningFor` yet. (5) The desktop cursor's `canGrab` call uses the mouse padding, which is right.

- Did: added milestone M10 and features F051–F057 for the 2026-11-26 design change (commit 02a4e0b): F051 the cap scores a flat 5 (`capPoints` tuning), a cap-farming measure in the benchmark (at most 10% at any water level and fixed aim, sigma 8), the design's fallback (a half-turn tip-over scores `capTipPoints` 1) only if the measure needs it, and the points rule re-checked with the cap at 5; F052 the cap celebration ('CAP! +5' drawn larger, its own burst cue, a `fanfare` voice); F053 the How to play card as a `HowTo` scene opened from the Title (button and H), with device-specific lines and the credit; F054 the two-bottle water-to-power pictures, baked; F055 the card from the Pause overlay and with H during play; F056 the card opening by itself at the first Play (`seenHowTo` in the save, same key and version); F057 the Flipsy name (Title, tab title, iPhone title, manifest) and the credit line on the Title. (4) The card is a Phaser scene, not DOM; the corner mute and fullscreen buttons aren't on it, and a press outside the card only closes it. (5) A card opened with H during play closes straight back to play; H does nothing on the round summary. (6) The card counts as seen when it opens, so a reload while it is open doesn't show it again. (7) The hook startScene('Game') never opens the card by itself; tests that press Play on the Title for other reasons preset `seenHowTo` in their helper. (8) Picture water levels about 0.2 and 0.8 with bars about 30% and 80% (not a hint about real powers).

- Did: `capPoints` (5) and `capTipPoints` (1) in tuning.ts; LandingJudge (outcome.ts) scores a cap `capPoints`, or `capTipPoints` when no full turn was completed (still a success and a landing); `expected.worstCap` {water, aim, chance} in difficulty.ts (17 levels x aims 0-100, sigma 8, counts only caps that score `capPoints`), printed by scripts/flip-windows.ts. Updated tests that assumed "turns, at least 1" (sweepPowers, landingJudging, flipRule e2e, the script test's line indices) and added tests for 5 / 1 / setTuning 7 / tip at 5, the worstCap measure and the 10% limit. ARCHITECTURE.md outcome row updated.
- Measured (npx tsx scripts/flip-windows.ts): tip-over at 5: worst cap chance 14.8% (water 80%, aim 14) > 10%, so the design's fallback applies. With the tip-over at 1 (kept): 9.9% (water 90%, aim 78). Expected landing rates unchanged: practiced 60.2%, new 44.8%. Points per 10, practiced vs bestFixedAim: before 6.02 vs 5.11; after (tip at 5) 6.04 vs 5.60; after (tip at 1, kept) 6.03 vs 5.60. Points rule holds. No physics value changed.
- Verified: `npm test` 966/966; `npm run verify`: format, typecheck, unit, build green, e2e 384 passed and 1 failed (mouse.spec.ts "the same with the real mouse": the throw did not reach flying in time, a timing flake unrelated to the cap; it passes alone, 6/6, on rerun).

- Next: reviewer: run `npx tsx scripts/flip-windows.ts capTipPoints=5` and confirm 14.8%, then the default for 9.9%.
